where the sign factor
(-)^{n_{\lambda,1}^{(2)}(g)}
comes from the swapping between
W_{M}
and
c_{L}
.
So, one conclude
\omega(g,g,g)=\operatorname{i}
.

VIII
Discussion

In conclusion, we have presented a comprehensive classification scheme for topological insulators based on tensor equations that capture symmetry transformation rules on internal legs.
By extracting quantum anomaly data from these equations, we have characterized the edge Hilbert spaces and symmetry actions associated with each topological phase.
Furthermore, by imposing symmetry constraints on local tensors, we have obtained variational wavefunctions that go beyond fixed-point solutions, providing a broader range of possibilities for simulating and understanding interacting topological phases.

Our work opens up several promising directions in the field of topological phases and fermionic tensor network states.
One natural extension is to incorporate space group symmetries to study higher-order topological insulator (HOTI) phases
Schindler et al. 2018
;
Rasmussen and Lu 2020
;
Song et al. 2020
;
Else and Thorngren 2019
;
Park et al. 2019
;
Chen et al. 2020
.
The lattice-based nature of FPEPS makes them well-suited for exploring the interplay between space and onsite symmetries.
Investigating whether Kasteleyn orientations impose additional constraints on the classification and characterization of HOTI phases would be particularly intriguing.
Additionally, the development of variational tensor wavefunctions for two-dimensional topological superconductors presents another avenue for future research
Tarantino and Fidkowski 2016
;
Ware et al. 2016
;
Wang and Gu 2018
;
Wang and Gu 2020
.
Exploring the possibility of decorating domain walls with Kitaev chains introduces new types of tensor equations and edge theories, offering novel insights into the properties of these systems.

An important and challenging direction is the realization of anomalous edge theories for topological insulator phases in lower-dimensional systems.
While bosonic SPT phases can be realized in systems with extended onsite symmetry groups, known as intrinsic gapless SPT (igSPT) phases
Thorngren et al. 2021
;
Wen and Potter 2023
, achieving the realization of edge theories for fermionic symmetry-protected topological (FSPT) phases with onsite symmetries and tensor products of local Hilbert spaces remains a significant challenge
Jones and Metlitski 2021
;
Metlitski 2019
.
The tensor equations derived in our work provide valuable insights for realizing such edge theories for two-dimensional topological insulator phases.

Expanding our framework to higher dimensions represents a captivating direction for future research.
Investigating tensor equations for three-dimensional FSPT phases could unveil new phenomena and enrich our understanding of higher-dimensional topological phases.
Of particular interest is the investigation of edge theories for three-dimensional topological insulator phases.
The recent connection between the half-filled Landau level problem
Willett et al. 1990
;
Halperin et al. 1993
, a renowned platform for studying non-Fermi liquid behavior, and the boundary of a three-dimensional time-reversal symmetric topological insulator with time-reversal acting as a particle-hole symmetry has opened up exciting possibilities
Lee 1998
;
Son 2015
;
Barkeshli et al. 2015
;
Wang and Senthil 2015
;
Metlitski and Vishwanath 2016
;
Wang and Senthil 2016
.
However, it remains an open question whether such systems can be effectively realized using onsite symmetries and tensor product structures of local Hilbert spaces in two dimensions.
Deriving tensor equations for these three-dimensional topological insulator phases holds great promise, as it could provide a framework for simulating their boundary theory.
This, in turn, opens up the possibility of simulating intriguing phenomena, such as the half-filled Landau level problem, on novel two-dimensional systems.

Furthermore, to enhance the practical applicability of our method and facilitate the exploration of topological insulator phases in correlated models, it is crucial to develop efficient numerical methods and algorithms
Orús and Vidal 2009
;
Corboz et al. 2010
;
Wu et al. 2020
;
Mortier et al. 2022
specifically tailored to fermionic tensor network states.
Our framework inherently reduces the number of variational parameters needed in numerical simulations, leading to improved computational efficiency.
By combining the power of our scheme with advanced numerical techniques, we may overcome the challenges posed by interaction effects, leading to the discovery of TI phases and a deeper understanding of their properties in strongly correlated systems.

We would like to thank Zheng-Cheng Gu, Chenjie Wang, and Chong Wang for helpful discussions. The work is supported by MOST grant No. 2022YFA1403900, NSFC No. 12104451, NSFC No. 11920101005, and funds from Strategic Priority Research Program of CAS No. XDB28000000.

Supplementary Material

I
Brief introduction to symmetric fermionic tensor network states

In this appendix, we review the fundamentals of fermionic tensor network states
Kraus et al. 2010
;
Bultinck et al. 2017b
;
Bultinck et al. 2017a
, and fix our notations used in the main text.

I.1
Fermionic tensors and tensor contraction

Building blocks of fermionic tensor networks are fermionic tensors, which live in fermionic tensor product (labeled as
\otimes_{f}
) of legs.
Legs with inward/outward arrows are local fermion Hilbert spaces for ket/bra states, where the parity of state
\lvert i\rangle/\langle i\rvert
is
(-1)^{\left\lvert i\right\rvert}
with
\left\lvert i\right\rvert\in\{0,1\}
.
Exchanging states of two legs gives
-1
if these two states are both parity odd:

\displaystyle\lvert i\rangle_{a}\otimes_{f}\lvert j\rangle_{b}=(-1)^{\left\lvert i\right\rvert\left\lvert j\right\rvert}\lvert j\rangle_{b}\otimes_{f}\lvert i\rangle_{a}

(S1)

As an example of fermionic tensors, let us consider tensor
\hat{T}
with three legs, say
a,b,c
:

\displaystyle\hat{T}=(T_{abc})_{ijk}\lvert i\rangle_{a}\otimes_{f}\lvert j\rangle_{b}\otimes_{f}\lvert k\rangle_{c}

(S2)

Leg indices
abc
are sometimes ignored when there is no confusion.
We may also omit
\otimes_{f}
’s and use a more compact form
\hat{T}=T_{ijk}\lvert ijk\rangle
.

Ket and bra states can be contracted using
{\rm{fTr}}
, defined as

\displaystyle{\rm{fTr}}[\langle i\rvert\otimes_{f}\lvert j\rangle]=(-)^{\left\lvert i\right\rvert\left\lvert j\right\rvert}{\rm{fTr}}[\lvert j\rangle\otimes_{f}\langle i\rvert]=\delta_{ij}

(S3)

It is noteworthy that the order of contracted states matters as extra
-1
may be produced.
Generalization to tensor contractions is straightforward.
As shown in Fig.
S1
, for two fermionic tensors
\hat{M}=M^{ijk}\langle i\rvert_{a}\langle j\rvert_{b}\langle k\rvert_{c}
,
\hat{N}=N_{lm}\lvert l\rangle_{b}\lvert m\rangle_{d}
,

\displaystyle{\rm{fTr}}_{b}[\hat{M}\otimes_{f}\hat{N}]\equiv(-1)^{\left\lvert j\right\rvert\left\lvert k\right\rvert}M^{ijk}N_{lm}\delta_{jl}\langle i\rvert\langle k\rvert\lvert m\rangle

(S4)

We may omit cumbersome
{\rm{fTr}}
’s and
\otimes_{f}
’s and use
\hat{M}\cdot\hat{N}
to represent tensor contraction.

Figure S1:
Graphic representation of contraction between fermionic tensors
\hat{M}
and
\hat{N}
.
The outward legs are bra spaces and inward legs are ket spaces.
The intersection of leg
b
and
c
indicates a possible extra minus sign due to exchanging fermions.

For local tensors in tensor network states, there are two types of legs: internal ones and physical ones.
States in physical legs is denoted by
\lvert\bullet\rangle/\langle\bullet\rvert
, while states in internal legs by
\left|\bullet\right)/\left(\bullet\right|
.
To get a physical wavefunction, all internal legs are contracted.
By fixing parity of all local tensors, physical wavefunctions have fixed parity
In this work, we focus on the case where all local tensors are
parity even
.

I.2
Gauge transformation and symmetries of FPEPS

We consider a particular type of fermionic tensor network – fermionic projected entangled-pair state(s) (FPEPS)
Schuch et al. 2011
.
As shown in Fig.
S2
(a), we focus on FPEPS with both site tensors
\hat{T}_{s}
and bond tensors
\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}
, where
s,s^{\prime}
label neighbouring site coordinates.
Without loss of generality, we assume that internal legs of site tensors all point inward (ket spaces), while those of bond tensors point outward (bra spaces).
Physical wavefunction then reads
\lvert\Psi\rangle={\rm{fTr}}\left[\mathbb{B}\otimes_{f}\mathbb{T}\right]
, where
\mathbb{T}=\bigotimes_{s}\mathop{{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}}\hat{T}_{s}
and
\mathbb{B}=\bigotimes_{\langle ss^{\prime}\rangle}\mathop{{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}}\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}

Note that as all tensors are parity even, different orders of tensor contraction give the same state.

Figure S2:
(a) A bond tensor
\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}
and its neighbouring site tensors. (b) A
3\times 3
FPEPS on square lattice with boundary legs. (c)
\mathrm{IGG}
invariance of a single site tensor.

Representation of physical wavefunction
\lvert\Psi\rangle
by FPEPS is far from unique.
In particular, two FPEPS represent the same wavefunction if they are related by some gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\lvert\psi\rangle={\rm{fTr}}\left[\mathbb{B}\otimes_{f}\mathbb{T}\right]={\rm{fTr}}\left[\mathbb{B}\otimes_{f}\mathbb{W}^{-1}\otimes_{f}\mathbb{W}\otimes_{f}\mathbb{T}\right]

(S5)

Here,
\mathbb{W}
and
\mathbb{W}^{-1}
are tensor products of gauge transformation
W
’s on internal legs:

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}

\displaystyle=W_{(s_{1}\alpha_{1})}\otimes_{f}W_{(s_{1}\alpha_{2})}\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}W_{(s_{1}\alpha_{m})}\otimes_{f}W_{(s_{2}\alpha_{1})}\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}W_{(s_{n}\alpha_{m})}~,

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}^{-1}

\displaystyle=W^{-1}_{(s_{n}\alpha_{m})}\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{(s_{n}\alpha_{1})}\otimes_{f}\cdots W^{-1}_{(s_{n-1}\alpha_{m})}\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{(s_{1}\alpha_{1})}

(S6)

where
(s\alpha)
labels internal leg, and

\displaystyle W_{(s\alpha)}\left|i\right)_{(s\alpha)}=\sum_{b}[W_{(s\alpha)}]_{ji}\left|j\right)_{(s\alpha)}~,\quad\left(i\right|_{(s\alpha)}W^{-1}_{(s\alpha)}=\sum_{j}\left(j\right|_{(s\alpha)}\left[W_{(s\alpha)}^{-1}\right]_{ij}

(S7)

W
’s in general do not have fixed parity, and thus permuting
W
’s and
W^{-1}
’s may lead to fermion swapping gate.

For the case where
\lvert\Psi\rangle
is invariant under symmetry
g
, we assume that
g
-action on physical legs can be pushed to gauge transformation on internal legs of local tensors:

\displaystyle U_{s}(g)\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{\alpha}\mathop{{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}}W_{(s\alpha)}(g)\right)\cdot\hat{T}_{s}=\hat{T}_{s}~,\quad\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}\cdot W^{-1}_{(s\alpha)}(g)\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{(s^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime})}(g)=\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}

(S8)

where
A\cdot B
means
{\rm{fTr}}[A\otimes_{f}B]
.
The above equations give symmetry constraints for local tensors.
We remark that to get a symmetric wavefunction, orders of
W(g)
’s in the above equation are essential.
In particular, as we will show in Appendix
VII
, a valid order of
W(g)
’s gives a Kasteleyn orientation on the lattice.

As shown in Fig.
S2
(c), there exists a special kind of gauge transformation
\mathbb{D}
, which leaves every single tensor invariant:

\displaystyle\left(\bigotimes_{\alpha}\mathop{{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}}D_{(s\alpha)}\right)\cdot\hat{T}_{s}=\hat{T}_{s}~,\quad\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}\cdot D^{-1}_{(s\alpha)}\otimes_{f}D^{-1}_{(s^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime})}=\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}~.

(S9)

Such gauge transformation form invariant gauge group (
\mathrm{IGG}
).
In this work, we focus on the case where
D
’s are parity even.

Note that the group always have a trivial center
H
formed by phase factors
\chi_{(s\alpha)}
that satisfy
\prod_{(s\alpha)}\chi_{(s\alpha)}=1
.
In addition, if the
\mathrm{IGG}
is a
U(1)
group generated by
n_{D;(s\alpha)}
, namely,
D_{(s\alpha)}(\theta)=\exp[\operatorname{i}\theta n_{D;(s\alpha)}]
.
We then have
\left(\sum_{\alpha}n_{D;(s\alpha)}\right)\cdot\hat{T}^{s}=0

I.3
Plaquette
\mathrm{IGG}
and vanishing long-range entanglement

Beside global
\mathrm{IGG}
which is a set of gauge transformations acting on all internal legs, there exists another type of
\mathrm{IGG}
which we call plaquette
\mathrm{IGG}
Jiang and Ran 2017b
. A plaquette
\mathrm{IGG}
is from the decomposition of a global
\mathrm{IGG}

\displaystyle D_{(s\alpha)}=\lambda^{(a)}_{(s\alpha)}\cdot\lambda^{(\bar{a})}_{(s\alpha)}

(S10)

Here,
a=1/2
while
\bar{a}=2/1
. We assume that
\mathrm{IGG}
’s acting on different plaquettes are commutative. What’s more, we have the plaquette
\mathrm{IGG}
equation on a site tensor as

(S11)

For a bond tensor, the plaquette
\mathrm{IGG}
equation is

(S12)

Thus the action of plaquette
\mathrm{IGG}
is local but still keeps local tensors invariant. In this work, we assume all global
\mathrm{IGG}
’s have such plaquette decomposition.

Figure S3:
Any
\mathrm{IGG}
element can be decomposed to plaquette IGG.

Nontrivial global
\mathrm{IGG}
element often leads to topological ground state degeneracy.
To see this, let us consider a FPEPS with periodic boundary condition on square lattice:

\displaystyle\lvert\Psi\rangle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}~,

where the bond tensors, physical legs and leg orientations are omitted for brevity.
If such tensor network has a nontrivial
\mathrm{IGG}
element, we can insert “
\mathrm{IGG}
loops” in internal legs, which leaves the wavefunction invariant:

\displaystyle\lvert\Psi\rangle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}~=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}~.

However, by inserting non-contractible loops of
\mathrm{IGG}
action, we in general obtain a different state as

\displaystyle\lvert\Psi_{v}\rangle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}.

(S13)

\lvert\Psi_{v}\rangle
can’t be distinguished with
\lvert\Psi\rangle
using local operators, as the noncontratible loop can move freely.
Thus, if
\lvert\psi\rangle
is a ground state of a local Hamiltonian, a nontrivial
\mathrm{IGG}
indicates that the Hamiltonian has topological ground state degeneracy.
In other words,
\lvert\psi\rangle
is a long-range entangled state.

However, the plaquette decomposition of the
\mathrm{IGG}
element kills the long-range entanglement.
To see this, we apply Eq. (
S11
) for
\lvert\Psi_{v}\rangle
in Eq. (
S13
), and obtain

\displaystyle\lvert\Psi_{v}\rangle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}~=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}~=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{{\hbox{{}}}}}}}$}~=\lvert\Psi\rangle

In other words, the non-contractible loop the
\mathrm{IGG}
element acts trivially on the tensor network state if the
\mathrm{IGG}
element has plaquette decomposition.
Thus, we obtain an SRE state.

II
Cocycle and coboundary conditions of
\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})

II.1
3-cocycle condition

In the following, by calculating
\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})
in two different ways, we will derive the consistent conditions for
\omega
as in Eq. (
48
).

Firstly, we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\left[\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\right]\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\omega(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\left[{}^{U_{M}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{3},g_{4})\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\right]\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})[\omega(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})]^{1-2s(g_{1})}\cdot{}^{\Omega(g_{1},g_{2})\exp[\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot n_{f}]U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{3},g_{4})\times

\displaystyle{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\exp\left[\operatorname{i}\cdot(-1)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})\right]\cdot(-1)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3}g_{4})}\cdot\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})[\omega(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})]^{1-2s(g_{1})}\cdot

\displaystyle{}^{\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{3},g_{4})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

(S14)

Then above equation can also be calculated in another way:

\displaystyle\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{3},g_{4})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\cdot{}^{\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{3},g_{4})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})\,,

(S15)

Thus, by equating Eq. (
S14
) and (
S15
), we obtain Eq. (
48
).

II.2
coboundary condition

The left side of Eq. (
52
) is straightforward:

\beta(g_{1},g_{2})\beta(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{2})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{3})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})\,.

(S16)

The right side is

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\Sigma_{b}(g_{1}){}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}(\beta(g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}(g_{2})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{3})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{2}g_{3}))\Sigma_{b}^{-1}(g_{1})\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{1})

\displaystyle(\beta(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{2}g_{3})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}))

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})(-)^{\mu(g_{1})(\mu(g_{2})+\mu(g_{3})+\sigma(g_{2}g_{3})-\mu(g_{2}g_{3}))}(\beta(g_{2},g_{3}))^{1-2s(g_{1})}\cdot\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\cdot

\displaystyle{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\left(\Sigma_{a}(g_{2})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3})\right)\beta(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\Sigma^{-1}_{a}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})(-)^{\mu(g_{1})\tilde{\sigma}(g_{2},g_{3})}(\beta(g_{2},g_{3}))^{1-2s(g_{1})}\beta(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\cdot\left({}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{2})\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\left({}^{\Omega(g_{1},g_{2})\exp\{i\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})n_{f}\}U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{3})\right)\cdot\left({}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3})\right)\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})(-)^{\mu(g_{1})\tilde{\sigma}(g_{2},g_{3})}(\beta(g_{2},g_{3}))^{1-2s(g_{1})}\beta(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\cdot\left({}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\exp\{i(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\mu(g_{3})\}(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\mu(g_{3})}\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\left({}^{U_{M}{g_{1}g_{2}}K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{3})\right)\cdot\Omega^{-1}_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\omega^{-1}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})

\displaystyle\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega^{-1}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})(-)^{\mu(g_{1})\tilde{\sigma}(g_{2},g_{3})+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\mu(g_{3})}(\beta(g_{2},g_{3}))^{1-2s(g_{1})}\beta(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\exp\{i(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\mu(g_{3})\}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{a}(g_{1})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{2})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot{}^{U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\Sigma_{a}(g_{3})\cdot\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\Sigma_{a}^{-1}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})\,.

(S17)

By comparing the above two equations, we obtain the coboundary condition Eq. (
53
):

\tilde{\omega}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})=\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\frac{\beta(g_{1},g_{2})\beta(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})}{\beta(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})(\beta(g_{2},g_{3}))^{1-2s(g_{1})}}(-)^{\mu(g_{1})\tilde{\sigma}(g_{2},g_{3})+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\mu(g_{3})}\exp\{-i(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\mu(g_{3})\}\,.

(S18)

III
Additional details and analysis on tensor equations

III.1
Action of
g_{1}g_{2}
on
n_{f}

In this part, we will prove Eq. (
72
) and Eq. (
73
).
Similar as in Section
V.1
, we start from a region
A
, and its boundary Hilbert space defined by projector
P_{edge}
.
Let us consider action of
n_{f}
on a single leg
j
together with action of
g_{1}g_{2}
on
\partial A
:

\displaystyle W_{\partial A}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot W_{\partial A}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}\cdot n_{f;j}\cdot P_{edge}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{f;j}+(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}n_{D;j}(g_{1})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-21.78004pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.62398pt}}_{{\kern-17.23509pt\kern 17.62398pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-19.13286pt{g_{1}}\kern 14.97679pt}}_{{\kern-14.5879pt\kern 14.97679pt}}}\right]\cdot W_{\partial A}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot W_{\partial A}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}\cdot P_{edge}

(S19)

where
W_{\partial A}(g)\equiv\bigotimes\mathop{{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}}_{,j\in\partial A}W_{j}(g)
, and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.84721pt{g}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.00688pt{g}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}\equiv W_{\partial A}(g)K^{s(g)}\circ n_{f;j}
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle W_{\partial A}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot n_{f;j}\cdot P_{edge}=\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{f;j}+n_{D;j}(g_{1}g_{2})\right]\cdot W_{\partial A}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot P_{edge}

(S20)

Note that
n_{f;j}
commute with
n_{\lambda}
’s, and
P_{edge}
is defined by
n_{\lambda}
’s as in Eq. (
125
) and Eq. (
127
).
Thus, we have
n_{f;j}\cdot P_{edge}=P_{edge}\cdot n_{f;j}
.
Together with the following equation indicated by Eq.(
129
) where

\displaystyle U_{edge}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\equiv P_{edge}W(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot=U_{edge}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot U_{edge}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}=P_{edge}W_{\partial A}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot W_{\partial A}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}\cdot\,,

(S21)

we conclude that Eq. (
S19
) and Eq. (
S20
) are equal to each other as in Eq. (
72
).
And by equating these two equations, we have

\displaystyle\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{f;j}+(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}n_{D;j}(g_{1})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-21.78004pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.62398pt}}_{{\kern-17.23509pt\kern 17.62398pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-19.13286pt{g_{1}}\kern 14.97679pt}}_{{\kern-14.5879pt\kern 14.97679pt}}}\right]\cdot P_{edge}=\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{f;j}+n_{D;j}(g_{1}g_{2})\right]\cdot P_{edge}

(S22)

In other words,
\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{f;j}+(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}n_{D;j}(g_{1})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-21.78004pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.62398pt}}_{{\kern-17.23509pt\kern 17.62398pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-19.13286pt{g_{1}}\kern 14.97679pt}}_{{\kern-14.5879pt\kern 14.97679pt}}}\right]
and
\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{f;j}+n_{D;j}(g_{1}g_{2})\right]
differ up to an IGG element.
However, these two terms only act on a single leg, thus they can only differ by a single-leg IGG, which we always set to be trivial.
Thus, we conclude

\displaystyle(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}n_{D;j}(g_{1})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.52222pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.64816pt}}_{{\kern-26.25928pt\kern 26.64816pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-21.78004pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.62398pt}}_{{\kern-17.23509pt\kern 17.62398pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{D;j}(g_{2})^{{\kern-19.13286pt{g_{1}}\kern 14.97679pt}}_{{\kern-14.5879pt\kern 14.97679pt}}}=n_{D;j}(g_{1}g_{2})

(S23)

as in Eq. (
73
).

III.2
Commutation relation between
n_{D}
’s

As assumed in the main text,
[n_{D,j}(g_{1}),\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{D,j}(g_{2})]=0
.
From Eq. (
73
), we conclude that
[n_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{1}),n_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{1}g_{2})]=0
, so

[n_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{1}),n_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{2})]=0,~~\forall g_{1,2}\in G_{b}\,.

(S24)

In addition,
\forall g_{1,2,3}\in G_{b}
,

\displaystyle[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{2}),\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-14.58032pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-14.58032pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-11.14217pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-10.30183pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{3})]=[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{2}),n_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})]=0

(S25)

Therefore

[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{2}),n_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{3})]=0,~~\forall g_{1,2,3}\in G_{b}\,.

(S26)

Acting
g_{4}\in G_{b}
to the equation above,

\displaystyle[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{4}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{4}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{4}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{4}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{3}),\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-14.58032pt{g_{4}g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-14.58032pt{g_{4}g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-11.14217pt{g_{4}g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{4}g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-10.30183pt{g_{4}g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{2})]=0\,,

so, we reach the final conclusion

[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{2}),\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{3}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{3}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{3}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{3}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{{}_{D,j}}(g_{4})]=0,~~\forall g_{1,2,3,4}\in G_{b}\,.

(S27)

III.3
Details about
D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\Lambda^{(a)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})

In this part, we try to derive expressions for
\Lambda^{(a)}
’s presented in Eq. (
98
).
We first express
D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})
as

\displaystyle D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}}\beta_{1}^{m_{1}}\beta_{2}^{m_{2}}\cdot d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})

(S28)

Inserting the above decomposition into Eq. (
92
), we obtain

\displaystyle W_{m_{1}}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot W_{m_{2}}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}=d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\exp\left[\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot n_{f}\right]\cdot W_{m^{\prime}}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}

(S29)

with
m^{\prime}=(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}\cdot m_{1}+m_{2}
.

We now try to identify domain and image of
d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})
.
According to Eq. (
69
), we have

\displaystyle n_{D}(g)\cdot W_{m}(g)

\displaystyle=m\cdot W_{m}(g)\,.

(S30)

Namely, image of
W_{m}(g)
is identified as subspace with
n_{D;s\alpha}(g)=m
(labeled as
\mathbb{H}_{D;m}(g)
).
Defining
p_{D;m}(g)
as the projector to
\mathbb{H}_{D;m}(g)
, we have

\displaystyle n_{D}(g)=\sum_{m}m\cdot p_{D;m}(g)

(S31)

Thus,
\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{1}}(g)\cap\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{2}}(g)=\emptyset
for
m_{1}\neq m_{2}
.

From Eq. (
S29
),
d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})
is a mapping from
\mathbb{H}_{D;m^{\prime}}(g_{1}g_{2})
to
\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.2772pt{g_{1}}\kern 31.40315pt}}_{{\kern-31.01427pt\kern 31.40315pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.2772pt{g_{1}}\kern 31.40315pt}}_{{\kern-31.01427pt\kern 31.40315pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})^{{\kern-26.33139pt{g_{1}}\kern 22.17532pt}}_{{\kern-21.78644pt\kern 22.17532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})^{{\kern-23.43565pt{g_{1}}\kern 19.27959pt}}_{{\kern-18.8907pt\kern 19.27959pt}}}
, where
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-10.31845pt{g_{1}}\kern 5.4444pt}}_{{\kern-5.05551pt\kern 5.4444pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-10.31845pt{g_{1}}\kern 5.4444pt}}_{{\kern-5.05551pt\kern 5.4444pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-7.96718pt{g_{1}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-6.8783pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})
labels space with
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{D}(g_{2})=m_{2}
.
From Eq. (

) as well as Eq. (

), we obtain

\displaystyle p_{D;m}(g_{1}g_{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}m_{1}\\
+m_{2}=m\end{subarray}}p_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-6.62138pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.46532pt}}_{{\kern-2.07643pt\kern 2.46532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-5.917pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.76094pt}}_{{\kern-1.37206pt\kern 1.76094pt}}}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})

(S32)

or equivalently

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{D;m}(g_{1}g_{2})=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}m_{1}\\
+m_{2}=m\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-10.31845pt{g_{1}}\kern 5.4444pt}}_{{\kern-5.05551pt\kern 5.4444pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-10.31845pt{g_{1}}\kern 5.4444pt}}_{{\kern-5.05551pt\kern 5.4444pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-7.96718pt{g_{1}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-6.8783pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}}{{}_{D;m_{2}}}(g_{2})\right)\,.

(S33)

Here, we simply assume
d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})
to be an automorphism in
\mathbb{H}_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-10.31845pt{g_{1}}\kern 5.4444pt}}_{{\kern-5.05551pt\kern 5.4444pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-10.31845pt{g_{1}}\kern 5.4444pt}}_{{\kern-5.05551pt\kern 5.4444pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-7.96718pt{g_{1}}\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\mathbb{H}^{{\kern-6.8783pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}}{{}_{D;m_{2}}}(g_{2})
, namely,

\displaystyle d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\big[p_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-6.62138pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.46532pt}}_{{\kern-2.07643pt\kern 2.46532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-5.917pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.76094pt}}_{{\kern-1.37206pt\kern 1.76094pt}}}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})\big]\cdot d_{m_{1}m_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\big[p_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-6.62138pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.46532pt}}_{{\kern-2.07643pt\kern 2.46532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-5.917pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.76094pt}}_{{\kern-1.37206pt\kern 1.76094pt}}}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})\big]

(S34)

Similar as Eq. (
S28
), we express
\Lambda_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})
as

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(a)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}}\beta_{1}^{l_{1}}\beta_{2}^{l_{2}}\cdot\lambda^{(a)}_{l_{1}l_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})

(S35)

Defining
p^{(a)}_{\lambda;l}(g)
as projector to space with
n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g)=l
(labeled as
\mathbb{H}^{(a)}_{\lambda;l}(g)
), we have

\displaystyle n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g)=\sum_{m}m\cdot p^{(a)}_{\lambda;m}(g)

(S36)

From relation in Eq. (

) where

, we have

\displaystyle p_{D;m}(g)=\sum_{l}p_{\lambda;l}^{(2)}(g)\cdot p_{\lambda;m-l}^{(1)}(g),

(S37)

We then have

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{m_{1}}\beta_{2}^{m_{2}}\cdot p_{D;m_{1}}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-6.62138pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.46532pt}}_{{\kern-2.07643pt\kern 2.46532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-5.917pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.76094pt}}_{{\kern-1.37206pt\kern 1.76094pt}}}_{D;m_{2}}(g_{2})=\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}}(-1)^{l_{2}(m_{1}-l_{1})}\Big[\beta_{1}^{l_{1}}\beta_{2}^{l_{2}}\cdot p_{\lambda;l_{1}}^{(2)}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;l_{2}}^{(2)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-30.8768pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.00275pt}}_{{\kern-25.61386pt\kern 26.00275pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;l_{2}}^{(2)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-30.8768pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.00275pt}}_{{\kern-25.61386pt\kern 26.00275pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;l_{2}}^{(2)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-22.0725pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.91643pt}}_{{\kern-17.52754pt\kern 17.91643pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;l_{2}}^{(2)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-19.56126pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.4052pt}}_{{\kern-15.01631pt\kern 15.4052pt}}}\Big]\cdot\Big[\beta_{1}^{m_{1}-l_{1}}\beta_{2}^{m_{2}-l_{2}}\cdot p_{\lambda;m_{1}-l_{1}}^{(1)}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;m-l_{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.8124pt{g_{1}}\kern 31.93834pt}}_{{\kern-31.54945pt\kern 31.93834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;m-l_{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.8124pt{g_{1}}\kern 31.93834pt}}_{{\kern-31.54945pt\kern 31.93834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;m-l_{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-26.3122pt{g_{1}}\kern 22.15613pt}}_{{\kern-21.76724pt\kern 22.15613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p_{\lambda;m-l_{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-23.80096pt{g_{1}}\kern 19.6449pt}}_{{\kern-19.25601pt\kern 19.6449pt}}}\Big]

Comparing this equation with Eq. (

as an automorphism in

, namely,

\displaystyle\lambda^{(a)}_{l_{1}l_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\big[p^{(a)}_{\lambda;l_{1}}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-6.62138pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.46532pt}}_{{\kern-2.07643pt\kern 2.46532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-5.917pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.76094pt}}_{{\kern-1.37206pt\kern 1.76094pt}}}^{(a)}_{\lambda;l_{2}}(g_{2})\big]\cdot\lambda^{(a)}_{l_{1}l_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\big[p^{(a)}_{\lambda;l_{1}}(g_{1})\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-8.9964pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.12234pt}}_{{\kern-3.73346pt\kern 4.12234pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-6.62138pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.46532pt}}_{{\kern-2.07643pt\kern 2.46532pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}p^{{\kern-5.917pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.76094pt}}_{{\kern-1.37206pt\kern 1.76094pt}}}^{(a)}_{\lambda;l_{2}}(g_{2})\big]

To make it more concise, we introduce

and

as

\displaystyle I_{\beta;(s\alpha)}(g)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m}\beta^{m}p_{D;(s\alpha)}(g,m)=(\beta)^{n_{D;(s\alpha)}(g)}

\displaystyle\iota_{\beta}^{(a)}(g)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\beta^{l}\cdot p^{(a)}_{\lambda;l}(g)=\beta^{n^{(a)}_{\lambda}(g)}\,.

(S40)

According to Eq. (
S37
), we get

\displaystyle I_{\beta}(g)=\iota_{\beta}^{(2)}(g)\cdot\iota_{\beta}^{(1)}(g)\,,

(S41)

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{}}}}}}$}\,,

(S42)

\displaystyle\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{}}}}}}$}\,,

\displaystyle\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{}}}}}}$}\,,

where

\displaystyle\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2})\equiv\bigoplus_{l_{1}l_{2}}\lambda^{(a)}_{l_{1},l_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})

Or equivalently,

\displaystyle[\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2}),\,n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{1})]=[\lambda^{(a)}_{l_{1},l_{2}}(g_{1},g_{2}),\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-30.07104pt{g_{1}}\kern 25.19699pt}}_{{\kern-24.8081pt\kern 25.19699pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-30.07104pt{g_{1}}\kern 25.19699pt}}_{{\kern-24.8081pt\kern 25.19699pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-20.74353pt{g_{1}}\kern 16.58746pt}}_{{\kern-16.19858pt\kern 16.58746pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-18.09634pt{g_{1}}\kern 13.94028pt}}_{{\kern-13.55139pt\kern 13.94028pt}}}]=0

’s, where

\displaystyle[\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2}),\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{4})^{{\kern-30.07104pt{g_{3}}\kern 25.19699pt}}_{{\kern-24.8081pt\kern 25.19699pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{4})^{{\kern-30.07104pt{g_{3}}\kern 25.19699pt}}_{{\kern-24.8081pt\kern 25.19699pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{4})^{{\kern-20.74353pt{g_{3}}\kern 16.58746pt}}_{{\kern-16.19858pt\kern 16.58746pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{3}}}}}n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g_{4})^{{\kern-18.09634pt{g_{3}}\kern 13.94028pt}}_{{\kern-13.55139pt\kern 13.94028pt}}}]=0\,,\quad\forall g_{j}\in G_{b}

From the definition of

’s in Eq (
S40
), we derive that

\displaystyle\iota_{\beta;(s\alpha)}^{(1)}(g)\cdot\iota_{\beta;(s\alpha+1)}^{(2)}(g)\cdot\hat{T}_{s}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}_{s}

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}\cdot\iota_{\beta;(s\alpha)}^{(a)}(g)\cdot\iota_{\beta;(s^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime})}^{(\bar{a})}(g)

), it is straightforward to see that

’s belong to plaquette IGG, namely,

\displaystyle\lambda_{(s\alpha)}^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2})\otimes_{f}\lambda_{(s\alpha+1)}^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\hat{T}_{s}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}_{s}

\displaystyle=\hat{B}_{ss^{\prime}}

IV
\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2})
and fermionic three cocycle data

In this appendix, we first use the associativity relation in Eq. (
104
) and (
106
) to derive the consistent conditions for
\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2})
.
Furthermore, by considering process related to four symmetry actions, we obtain pentagon equations which give fermionic three-cocycle conditions.

\lambda(g_{1},g_{2})
’s

(S51)

The red move does not contribute any additional factor.
Two blue moves contain two
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-35.04959pt{g_{1}}\kern 30.17554pt}}_{{\kern-29.78665pt\kern 30.17554pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-35.04959pt{g_{1}}\kern 30.17554pt}}_{{\kern-29.78665pt\kern 30.17554pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-24.19955pt{g_{1}}\kern 20.04349pt}}_{{\kern-19.6546pt\kern 20.04349pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-21.1792pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.02313pt}}_{{\kern-16.63425pt\kern 17.02313pt}}}
terms, and both move across
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-17.13425pt{g_{1}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}
, thus no additional factor either.
The only additional factor comes from moving
\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})
across
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-17.13425pt{g_{1}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}
,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{3}})^{{\kern-32.71008pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{3}})^{{\kern-32.71008pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{3}})^{{\kern-23.48157pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{3}})^{{\kern-21.1792pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}

, which contributes

(-)^{{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{2})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-11.71613pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-11.71613pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.38725pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.78702pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{3})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{2}g_{3}))}=(-)^{{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}\,.

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})}\right)\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{2},\beta_{3})}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-50.14621pt{g_{1}}\kern 45.27216pt}}_{{\kern-44.88327pt\kern 45.27216pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{2},\beta_{3})}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-50.14621pt{g_{1}}\kern 45.27216pt}}_{{\kern-44.88327pt\kern 45.27216pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{2},\beta_{3})}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-36.27649pt{g_{1}}\kern 32.12042pt}}_{{\kern-31.73154pt\kern 32.12042pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{2},\beta_{3})}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-32.39053pt{g_{1}}\kern 28.23447pt}}_{{\kern-27.84558pt\kern 28.23447pt}}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-35.04959pt{g_{1}}\kern 30.17554pt}}_{{\kern-29.78665pt\kern 30.17554pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-35.04959pt{g_{1}}\kern 30.17554pt}}_{{\kern-29.78665pt\kern 30.17554pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-24.19955pt{g_{1}}\kern 20.04349pt}}_{{\kern-19.6546pt\kern 20.04349pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{3}}({g_{2}g_{3}})^{{\kern-21.1792pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.02313pt}}_{{\kern-16.63425pt\kern 17.02313pt}}}\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{3})}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})

By rearranging

’s on both sides, we obtain

\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2})\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})=\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})(-)^{{n_{\lambda}^{(1)}(g_{1})}\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})}\right)\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{(1)}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-40.46278pt{g_{1}}\kern 35.58873pt}}_{{\kern-35.19984pt\kern 35.58873pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{(1)}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-40.46278pt{g_{1}}\kern 35.58873pt}}_{{\kern-35.19984pt\kern 35.58873pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{(1)}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-28.05046pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.8944pt}}_{{\kern-23.50551pt\kern 23.8944pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{(1)}(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-24.32005pt{g_{1}}\kern 20.16399pt}}_{{\kern-19.7751pt\kern 20.16399pt}}}\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\,,

(S56)

IV.2
3-cocycle data from
\lambda(g_{1},g_{2})
’s

In this part, we will derive the consistent condition for
\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})
by considering
\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})
.
There are two ways to calculate this expression, which form the pentagon equation (c.f. Appendix
II
).
Using Eq. (
S54
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\right)\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\right)\cdot\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\cdot{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\right)\cdot\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Bigg(^{{\kern-28.40508pt{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.11115pt}}_{{\kern-9.72226pt\kern 10.11115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Bigg(^{{\kern-28.40508pt{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.11115pt}}_{{\kern-9.72226pt\kern 10.11115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Bigg(^{{\kern-26.39792pt{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.11115pt}}_{{\kern-9.72226pt\kern 10.11115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Bigg(^{{\kern-26.39792pt{g_{1}K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.11115pt}}_{{\kern-9.72226pt\kern 10.11115pt}}}\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{2})}\right)\Bigg)\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-14.38007pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-14.38007pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.05936pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-10.24269pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{3},g_{4})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

(S57)

Another fusion process gives

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2})}\right)\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-14.38007pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-14.38007pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.05936pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-10.24269pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{3},g_{4})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2})}\right)\cdot\frac{(-)^{n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}\cdot\left(\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-14.38007pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-14.38007pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.05936pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}g_{2}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-10.24269pt{g_{1}g_{2}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{3},g_{4})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})

(S58)

Comparing Eq. (
S57
) and (
S58
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})[\omega^{\prime}(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})]^{1-2s(g_{1})}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})(-\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})+\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})+\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4}))}(-)^{\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{2})-n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1}g_{2}))}

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})(\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})+\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})-\alpha(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4}))}~\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{2})-n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1}g_{2}))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{((-)^{{\rho}(g_{2})}n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{2})-n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1}g_{2}))\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}~\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})((-)^{s(g_{1})+\tilde{\rho}(g_{2})}n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})+(-)^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{2})-n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1}g_{2})))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}~\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}~\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})}

(S59)

Thus, we recover Eq. (
109
).

V
Details about solutions of tensor equations

In this appendix, for a given symmetry group
G_{f}
and fermionic three cocycle data
(\sigma,\omega)
, we construct
n_{f}
and
W(g)
based on this data, extract IGG/IGA
n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g)
and
\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2})
from them, and verify that they indeed satisfy tensor equations presented in Eq. (
111
) and (
115
).

As in the main text,
G_{b}
here is a finite group with order
\left\lvert G_{b}\right\rvert
.
Dimension of an internal leg equals
\left\lvert G_{b}\right\rvert^{2}
, whose basis are labeled as
\lvert l,r\rangle
, where
l,r\in G_{b}
.
Internal legs points from bond tensors to site tensors.
We focus on FPEPS on a bipartite lattice, where sublattice indices are labeled as
u,v
.
In the following, we will consider these two sublattices separately.

V.1
u-
sublattice

We first examine symmetry action on internal legs on
u-
sublattice.
We will ignore internal leg index
(u\alpha)
for simplicity.
Fermion charge number operator acts as

\displaystyle n_{f}\lvert l,r\rangle=-\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)\lvert l,r\rangle

(S60)

where
\sigma
is the twisted 2-cocycle that satisfies Eq. (
47
).

g\in G_{b}
is represented as
W(g)
on internal legs, which acts as

\displaystyle W(g)\lvert l,r\rangle=(-)^{\sigma(g,r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}\cdot\omega^{-1}(g,r,r^{-1}l)\lvert gl,gr\rangle

(S61)

Here,
\omega
is the given fermionic three cocycle that satisfies Eq. (
48
)

By acting
W(g)
on
n_{f}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.84721pt{g}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.00688pt{g}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{f}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}-\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)\lvert gl,gr\rangle\langle gl,gr\rvert=\sum_{l,r}-\sigma(g^{-1}r,r^{-1}l)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\rho(g)}\cdot\left(-\sigma(g,g^{-1}r)+\sigma(g,g^{-1}l)-\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)\right)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,.

(S62)

where we use Eq. (
47
) to get equation on the second line.
Since
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.84721pt{g}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.00688pt{g}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{f}=(-)^{\rho(g)}n_{f}+n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g)+n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g)
,
By comparing with the first line in Eq. (
111
) and Eq. (
112
), we identify

\displaystyle n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g)=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\rho(g)}\sigma(g,g^{-1}l)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,;\quad n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g)=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\rho(g)+1}\sigma(g,g^{-1}r)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,.

(S63)

It is now straightforward to check the first line in Eq. (
115
) by inserting the above definition of
n_{\lambda}^{(a)}(g)
and using Eq. (
47
).

Instead of extracting
\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2})
from group relation in Eq. (
111
), (
112
), and Eq. (
113
), here we directly construct
\lambda^{(a)}(g_{1},g_{2})
, and show they satisfy all tensor equations.
We start from expression for
\lambda^{(2)}
, which reads

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{1}^{-1}l)}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}l)}\exp\left[\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{1}^{-1}l)\right]\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

(S64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}l)}\exp\left[\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)\right]\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert\,.

where
\omega^{\prime}
are related to
\omega
by Eq. (
110
).

We are now ready to verify that such
\lambda^{(2)}
satisfies Eq. (
107
), where for later convenience, we rewrite it as

\frac{\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})}{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3})\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})}\cdot\frac{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}{(-)^{n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}=1

(S65)

We list all other terms in the above equations:

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}l)}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},l)}\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}l)\right\}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)}}{[\omega^{\prime}(g_{2},g_{3},l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})+\tilde{\rho}(g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)\right\}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},l)}\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)\right\}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{1})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)\right\}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

\displaystyle(-)^{n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

(S66)

Inserting above expressions into left-hand side of Eq. (
S65
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},l)[\omega^{\prime}(g_{2},g_{3},l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}l)\omega^{\prime}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},l)}\cdot\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)+\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}l)}}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)+\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)+\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}l)\right\}}{\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})+\tilde{\rho}(g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)\right\}}

\displaystyle\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{\exp\left\{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}l)+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})}}\right\}}{\exp\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})+\tilde{\rho}(g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}\right\}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{(-)^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{+\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}l)+\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})}}}}{(-)^{{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}l)}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{+\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)+\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)\right\}}}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)\right\}}}}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{3},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}l)}}}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rangle\langle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}l,g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}r\rvert=1

(S67)

where we use three cocycle condition defined in Eq. (
109
), and then merge terms with the same color by two cocycle condition.

Similarly,
\lambda^{(1)}
reads

\displaystyle\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2})=\sum_{l,r}\frac{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}r)}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}r)\sigma(g_{1},g_{1}^{-1}r)}}\exp\left\{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}r)\sigma(g_{1},g_{1}^{-1}r)\right\}\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

(S68)

Now let us check that Eq. (
S61
), (
S64
) and Eq. (
S68
) satisfy the second line in Eq. (
111
), where we rewrite here for convenience:

W_{\beta_{1}}(g_{1})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-17.55016pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.30833pt}}_{{\kern-4.91945pt\kern 5.30833pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-16.0335pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.79167pt}}_{{\kern-3.40279pt\kern 3.79167pt}}}_{\beta_{2}}(g_{2})=D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\exp[\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot n_{f}]\cdot W(g_{1}g_{2})\,,

(S69)

The left-hand side equals

\displaystyle\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})W(g_{1})\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-17.55016pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.30833pt}}_{{\kern-4.91945pt\kern 5.30833pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-16.0335pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.79167pt}}_{{\kern-3.40279pt\kern 3.79167pt}}}(g_{2})=\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern
