(S71)

Therefore, we should check the following equation:

\displaystyle W(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-35.48071pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 21.94972pt}}_{{\kern-21.56084pt\kern 21.94972pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-35.48071pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 21.94972pt}}_{{\kern-21.56084pt\kern 21.94972pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-26.12419pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 13.88237pt}}_{{\kern-13.49348pt\kern 13.88237pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-22.80067pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.55885pt}}_{{\kern-10.16997pt\kern 10.55885pt}}}=(-)^{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{2})[n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})+n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})]}\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})n_{f}}W(g_{1}g_{2})\,.

(S72)

Putting in the definition of
W(g)
and
\lambda^{(a)}
, we obtain the left hand side of Eq. (
S72
) as

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)\sigma(g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)}}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}}{[\omega(g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2},r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,,

(S73)

and the right hand side with the following lengthy expression

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},l)[\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)]}\cdot\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},l)}\cdot\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},r)(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)}\cdot\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}}{\omega(g_{1}g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)}

(S74)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}[\alpha(g_{2},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)-\alpha(g_{2},r)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)]\right\}\exp\left\{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)\right\}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,.

We now use the relation between
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
in Eq. (
110
), where the detailed derivations are presented in Sec.VI of SM
10
10
footnotemark:

10

:
The right-hand side of Eq. (
S72
) now becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}(-)^{[\sigma(g_{2},l)+\sigma(g_{2},r)]\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)+\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}\cdot\frac{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},r)}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},l)\omega(g_{1}g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})[\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},l)-\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)-\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)]}

\displaystyle\cdot\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}(-)^{[\sigma(g_{2},l)+\sigma(g_{2},r)]\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)+\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}\cdot\frac{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},r)\omega(g_{1},g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)[\omega(g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},l)\omega(g_{1}g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)}

\displaystyle\cdot\omega^{-1}(g_{1},g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)[\omega^{-1}(g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}\cdot\exp\left\{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)\right\}\cdot\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{[\sigma(g_{2},l)+\sigma(g_{2},r)]\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)}(-)^{[\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})]\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)[\omega(g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)\left[\sigma(g_{2},l)+\sigma(g_{2},r)+\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)\right]}(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)[\omega(g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)\sigma(g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)}(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(r,r^{-1}l)}}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2}r,r^{-1}l)[\omega(g_{2},r,r^{-1}l)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,,

(S75)

where we apply three cocycle condition for
\omega
to get the second equation.
Comparing the above equation with Eq. (
S73
), we thus prove Eq. (
S72
).

So, Eq. (
S61
) is indeed a solution for tensor equations with given fermionic three cocycle
(\sigma,\omega)
.

V.2
v-
sublattice

The construction for
W(g)
on internal legs of
v-
sublattice is similar to that on internal legs of
u-
sublattice presented in the previous part.
We also ignore internal leg index
(v\alpha)
for simplicity.

Fermion charge number operator reads

\displaystyle n_{f}=\sum_{l,r}\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

(S76)

where the sign difference comes from the opposite charges on different sublattices, and the exchange between
l
and
r
comes from the different definitions of local Hilbert space for different sublattices.

Internal leg
g
-action

\displaystyle W(g)=\sum_{l,r}\frac{\omega(g,l,l^{-1}r)}{(-)^{\sigma(g,r)\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)}}\lvert gl,gr\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,.

(S77)

By acting
W(g)
on
n_{f}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-7.56738pt{g}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.84721pt{g}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g}}}}n^{{\kern-4.00688pt{g}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{f}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}\sigma(g^{-1}l,l^{-1}r)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\rho(g)}\left(\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)+\sigma(g,g^{-1}l)-\sigma(g,g^{-1}r)\right)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,.

(S78)

Therefore we can assign

\displaystyle n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g)=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\rho(g)}\sigma(g,g^{-1}l)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,,\quad n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g)=\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\rho(g)+1}\sigma(g,g^{-1}r)\lvert l,r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,,

(S79)

which shares the same form as those for the
u-
sublattice.

\lambda(g_{1},g_{2})
’s for the
v
-sublattice can be taken as the same form as those for the
u-
sublattice, and Eq. (
S65
) will also be satisfied.
To see this, we point out that that for Eq. (
S65
), the only subtlety between
u-
and
v-
sublattice is
g_{1}-
action on
\lambda^{(2)}(g_{2},g_{3})
.
However, it is easy to check that such subtlety will not make any difference for these two sublattices, and therefore Eq. (
107
) works for
v-
sublattice as well.

Again we want to check that
W(g)
’s and
\lambda^{(a)}
’s proposed here satisfy Eq. (
S69
).
We shall repeat what we have done in the last subsection.
Fusion of two symmetry elements gives

\displaystyle W_{\beta_{1}}(g_{1})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-17.55016pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.30833pt}}_{{\kern-4.91945pt\kern 5.30833pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-16.0335pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.79167pt}}_{{\kern-3.40279pt\kern 3.79167pt}}}_{\beta_{2}}(g_{2})

\displaystyle=\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-17.13425pt{g_{1}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-17.13425pt{g_{1}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}W(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-35.48071pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 21.94972pt}}_{{\kern-21.56084pt\kern 21.94972pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-35.48071pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 21.94972pt}}_{{\kern-21.56084pt\kern 21.94972pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-26.12419pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 13.88237pt}}_{{\kern-13.49348pt\kern 13.88237pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-22.80067pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.55885pt}}_{{\kern-10.16997pt\kern 10.55885pt}}}

(S80)

where

\displaystyle W(g_{1})\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-35.48071pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 21.94972pt}}_{{\kern-21.56084pt\kern 21.94972pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-35.48071pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 21.94972pt}}_{{\kern-21.56084pt\kern 21.94972pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-26.12419pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 13.88237pt}}_{{\kern-13.49348pt\kern 13.88237pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W(g_{2})^{{\kern-22.80067pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 10.55885pt}}_{{\kern-10.16997pt\kern 10.55885pt}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,r}\frac{\omega(g_{1},g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)\sigma(g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)}}\cdot\frac{[\omega(g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,.

(S81)

For the right-hand side of Eq. (
S69
), we have

\displaystyle D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot n_{f}\}\cdot W(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{1}}({g_{1}})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(2)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-17.13425pt{g_{1}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-27.94714pt{g_{1}}\kern 23.07309pt}}_{{\kern-22.6842pt\kern 23.07309pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-19.43661pt{g_{1}}\kern 15.28055pt}}_{{\kern-14.89166pt\kern 15.28055pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota^{(1)}_{\beta_{2}}({g_{2}})^{{\kern-17.13425pt{g_{1}}\kern 12.97818pt}}_{{\kern-12.5893pt\kern 12.97818pt}}}\cdot(-)^{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{2})[n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})+n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})]}\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})n_{f}}\cdot W(g_{1}g_{2})

(S82)

in which

\displaystyle(-)^{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}_{\lambda}^{(2)}(g_{2})[n^{(2)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})+n^{(1)}_{\lambda}(g_{1})]}\cdot\lambda^{(2)}(g_{1},g_{2})\lambda^{(1)}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})n_{f}}\cdot W(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},l)[\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)]}\cdot\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},l)}\cdot\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},r)\cdot(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)}\cdot\frac{\omega(g_{1}g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)}}

(S83)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}[\alpha(g_{2},l)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)-\alpha(g_{2},r)\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)]\right\}\cdot\exp\left[\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)\right]\cdot\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2},r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},l)[\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)]}}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)}}\cdot\frac{\omega(g_{1}g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)\omega(g_{1},g_{2},r)}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},l)\omega(g_{1},g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)[\omega(g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}

\displaystyle\times\exp\left\{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)\right\}\cdot\omega(g_{1},g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)[\omega(g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2},r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

(S84)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},l)[\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}l)+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)]}}{(-)^{\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)[\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},r)+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})]}}\cdot\omega(g_{1},g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)[\omega(g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2},r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,r}\frac{\omega(g_{1},g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}r)\sigma(g_{2}l,l^{-1}r)}}\cdot\frac{[\omega(g_{2},l,l^{-1}r)]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},r)\sigma(l,l^{-1}r)}}\lvert g_{1}g_{2}l,g_{1}g_{2}r\rangle\langle l,r\rvert\,,

(S85)

which equals Eq. (
S81
).
So, we conclude that Eq. (
S69
) is also satisfied on
v-
sublattice.

VI
Details about
\Omega_{a/b}(g_{1},g_{2})

VI.1
Explicit constructions of
\Omega_{a/b}

In this section let us derive the explicit form of
\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})
.
The input of the derivation is only the tensor equations that we explained and introduced in Section
IV
.
For a symmetry action
\forall g\in G_{b}
, the MPO form of this symmetry operation on a segment of edge
M
(
1,\,2,\,\cdots,\,l
) is given by:

U_{M}(g)=P_{edge}\cdot d_{\beta}^{-1}{\rm{fTr}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}_{\beta}\left[w_{{}_{\beta,l+1}}(g)\otimes_{f}w_{{}_{\beta,L}}(g)\bigotimes_{j=1}^{l}\mathop{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}W_{\beta,j}(g)\right]\right]K^{s(g)}\cdot P_{edge}\,,

(S86)

in which

\displaystyle W_{\beta,j}(g)=I_{\beta,j}(g)\cdot W_{j}(g);~w_{{}_{\beta,L}}(g)=\iota_{\beta,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g)\cdot w_{L}(g);~w_{{}_{\beta,l+1}}(g)=\iota_{\beta,l+\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}(g)\cdot w_{l+1}(g)\,.

(S87)

The fusion of two symmetry actions on the same segment
M
is then

\displaystyle U_{M}(g_{1})\cdot U_{M}(g_{2})=P_{edge}\cdot d_{\beta}^{-1}{\rm{fTr}}\Bigg[\mathrm{Tr}_{\beta}\Bigg[

\displaystyle(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},l+1}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},l+1}}(g_{2}))\otimes_{f}(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},L}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},L}}(g_{2}))

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{j=1}^{l}\mathop{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}W_{\beta_{1},j}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-22.12823pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 8.59724pt}}_{{\kern-8.20836pt\kern 8.59724pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-17.55016pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.30833pt}}_{{\kern-4.91945pt\kern 5.30833pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}W^{{\kern-16.0335pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.79167pt}}_{{\kern-3.40279pt\kern 3.79167pt}}}_{\beta_{2},j}(g_{2})\Bigg]\Bigg]K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot P_{edge}\,.

(S88)

Locally, a fusion of two symmetry actions gives the IGG
D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),j}(g_{1},g_{2})
, that introduced in section
IV
Eq. (
92
)

W_{\beta_{1},j}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot W_{\beta_{2},j}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}=D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),j}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot n_{f,j}\}\cdot W_{j}(g_{1}g_{2})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\,,

(S89)

such fusion is then

\displaystyle U_{M}(g_{1})\cdot U_{M}(g_{2})=P_{edge}\cdot d_{\beta}^{-1}{\rm{fTr}}\Bigg[\mathrm{Tr}_{\beta}\Bigg[

\displaystyle(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},l+1}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},l+1}}(g_{2}))\otimes_{f}(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},L}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},L}}(g_{2}))

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{j=1}^{l}\mathop{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),j}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})n_{{}_{f,j}}\}\cdot W_{j}(g_{1},g_{2})\Bigg]\Bigg]K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot P_{edge}\,.

(S90)

By separating
D_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}(g_{1},g_{2})
’s into plaquette IGG
\Lambda
’s, most of the plaquette terms will be absorbed by
P_{edge}
, and consequently we can rewrite the such segment fusion as

\displaystyle U_{M}(g_{1})\cdot U_{M}(g_{2})=P_{edge}\cdot d_{\beta}^{-1}{\rm{fTr}}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{\beta}\Bigg[(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},l+1}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},l+1}}(g_{2}))\otimes_{f}(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},L}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},L}}(g_{2}))\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(1)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),l+\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha\,n_{{}_{f,M}}\}\cdot W_{j}(g_{1},g_{2})\Bigg]\Bigg]K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot P_{edge}\,,

(S91)

with
n_{{}_{f,M}}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}n_{{}_{f,i}}
.
For a symmetry group element
(g,\alpha)\in G_{f}
, its symmetry action on a segment of the edge is given by

\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha\,(n_{{}_{f,L}}+n_{{}_{f,l+1}})\}\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha\,n_{{}_{f,M}}\}\cdot U_{M}(g)=\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha\,n_{{}_{f,\overline{M}}}\}\cdot U_{M}(g)\,,

since we want to ask that
(g,0)\cdot(e,\alpha)=(g,(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g)}\alpha)
also holds on a segment, such that

\displaystyle\left(U_{M}(g)K^{s(g)}\right)\cdot\exp\{\operatorname{i}\alpha\,n_{{}_{f,\overline{M}}}\}\cdot\left(U_{M}(g)K^{s(g)}\right)^{-1}=\exp\{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g)}\alpha\,n_{{}_{f,\overline{M}}}\}\,.

(S92)

The fusion is then related to
U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})
as

\displaystyle U_{M}(g_{1})\cdot U_{M}(g_{2})=P_{edge}\cdot d_{\beta}^{-1}{\rm{fTr}}\Bigg[\mathrm{Tr}_{\beta}\Bigg[(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},l+1}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},l+1}}(g_{2}))\otimes_{f}(w_{{}_{\beta_{1},L}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},L}}(g_{2}))\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(1)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),l+\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})w^{-1}_{l+1}(g_{1}g_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\,(n_{{}_{f,L}}+n_{{}_{f,l+1}})}\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})n_{{}_{f,\overline{M}}}}\cdot U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})\Bigg]\Bigg]K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}\cdot P_{edge}\,.

(S93)

Finally we need to deal with additional boundary operators
w_{L}
’s and
w_{l+1}
’s. For
w_{L}
’s, we have

\displaystyle P_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot w_{{}_{\beta_{1},L}}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{{}_{\beta_{2},L}}(g_{2})=

\displaystyle P_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\iota_{\beta_{1},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{1})~\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\iota_{\beta_{1},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.56873pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.69467pt}}_{{\kern-26.30579pt\kern 26.69467pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.56873pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.69467pt}}_{{\kern-26.30579pt\kern 26.69467pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-23.66629pt{g_{1}}\kern 19.51022pt}}_{{\kern-19.12134pt\kern 19.51022pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-21.36392pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.20786pt}}_{{\kern-16.81897pt\kern 17.20786pt}}}w_{L}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}\,,

(S94)

which can be perfectly absorbed together with
\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})
,

\displaystyle P_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\iota_{\beta_{1},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.56873pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.69467pt}}_{{\kern-26.30579pt\kern 26.69467pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-31.56873pt{g_{1}}\kern 26.69467pt}}_{{\kern-26.30579pt\kern 26.69467pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-23.66629pt{g_{1}}\kern 19.51022pt}}_{{\kern-19.12134pt\kern 19.51022pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\iota_{\beta_{2},\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})^{{\kern-21.36392pt{g_{1}}\kern 17.20786pt}}_{{\kern-16.81897pt\kern 17.20786pt}}}\left(w_{L}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}\right)\Lambda^{(2)}_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left(w_{L}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}\right)\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\,.

(S95)

Thus we can conclude that
\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})
is

\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})=P_{edge}\cdot w_{L}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\,n_{{}_{f,L}}}\,\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot P_{edge}

(S96)

Similarly, we can repeat what we have done for
w_{l+1}
’s, and we got

\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})=P_{edge}\cdot(-)^{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.17117pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.40399pt}}_{{\kern-4.12622pt\kern 4.40399pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-5.35515pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.82304pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-4.75491pt{g_{1}}\kern 1.50058pt}}_{{\kern-1.22281pt\kern 1.50058pt}}}_{\lambda,l+\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}(g_{2})\,{n}_{\lambda,l+\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}(g_{1})}w_{l+1}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-19.5044pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.9734pt}}_{{\kern-5.58452pt\kern 5.9734pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-15.88167pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.63985pt}}_{{\kern-3.25096pt\kern 3.63985pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w^{{\kern-14.8417pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.59988pt}}_{{\kern-2.211pt\kern 2.59988pt}}}_{l+1}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{l+1}(g_{1}g_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\,n_{{}_{f,l+1}}}\,\lambda^{(1)}_{l+\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot P_{edge}\,.

(S97)

VI.2
Fermion number of
\Omega_{a/b}

We already know

\displaystyle[w_{L}(g),n_{{}_{f,L}}]_{\rho(g)}=n_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g)\cdot w_{L}(g)\,,

then the commutation between
w^{-1}_{L}(g)
and
n_{{}_{f,L}}
should be

\displaystyle[w^{-1}_{L}(g),n_{f,L}]_{\rho(g)}=-(-)^{\rho(g)}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-10.98404pt{g^{-1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-10.98404pt{g^{-1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.26387pt{g^{-1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.42354pt{g^{-1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g)\cdot w^{-1}_{L}(g)\,,

(S98)

such that

\displaystyle w^{-1}_{L}(g)w_{L}(g)n_{{}_{f,L}}w^{-1}_{L}(g)w_{L}(g)=n_{{}_{f,L}}

is satisfied.
The commutation between
\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})
and
n_{{}_{f,L}}
is given through

\displaystyle w_{L}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})\cdot n_{{}_{f,L}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{L}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{2})\cdot\left((-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\left(n_{{}_{f,L}}-\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-16.91364pt{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-16.91364pt{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-13.47548pt{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-12.63515pt{g_{2}^{-1}g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g)\right)\right)\cdot w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{L}(g_{1})\cdot\left((-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\left((-)^{\rho(g_{2})}n_{{}_{f,L}}+n_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})-\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-10.98404pt{g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-10.98404pt{g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-8.26387pt{g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}^{-1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.42354pt{g_{1}^{-1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}^{(1)}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1}g_{2})\right)\right)\cdot w_{L}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\left((-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}n_{{}_{f,L}}+(-)^{\rho(g_{2})}n_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{1})+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-9.81738pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.94333pt}}_{{\kern-4.55444pt\kern 4.94333pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-7.09721pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.94115pt}}_{{\kern-2.55226pt\kern 2.94115pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}n^{{\kern-6.25688pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.10081pt}}_{{\kern-1.71193pt\kern 2.10081pt}}}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{2})-n^{(1)}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1}g_{2})\right)\cdot w_{L}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n_{{}_{f,L}}-\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}))\cdot w_{L}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[w_{L}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2}),n_{{}_{f,L}}]=-\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})w_{L}(g_{1})w_{L}(g_{2})w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2}),n_{{}_{f,L}}]=-\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})\,.

(S99)

In the equations above, condition given by Eq. (
76
) is used.
Similarly for
\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})
we have

\displaystyle[\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}),n_{{}_{f,L}}]=+\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\,.

(S100)

Thus we recover the fermion number condition in Eq. (
44
).

VI.3
3-cocycle data
\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})

To obtain the condition of 3-cocycle
\omega
(Eq. (
46
)), a fusion of three symmetry actions to the same segment is needed:
\forall g_{1,2,3}\in G_{b}

\displaystyle U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot U_{M}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}\cdot U_{M}(g_{3})K^{s(g_{3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\Omega(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\left(\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})+\alpha(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\right)n_{{}_{f,M}}}\cdot U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})}\,,

(S101)

while we can also fuse
g_{2}
and
g_{3}
first:

\displaystyle U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}\cdot U_{M}(g_{2})K^{s(g_{2})}\cdot U_{M}(g_{3})K^{s(g_{3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-60.05484pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 29.79292pt}}_{{\kern-29.40404pt\kern 29.79292pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-60.05484pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 29.79292pt}}_{{\kern-29.40404pt\kern 29.79292pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-45.6601pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 19.60272pt}}_{{\kern-19.21384pt\kern 19.60272pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-41.73523pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.67786pt}}_{{\kern-15.28897pt\kern 15.67786pt}}}\cdot\Omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}\left((-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})+\alpha(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\right)n_{{}_{f,M}}}\cdot U_{M}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})K^{s(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})}\,.

(S102)

Equating Eq. (
S101
) and (
S102
), we obtain a condition that
\Omega
should satisfy:

\displaystyle\Omega(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\Omega(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot P_{edge}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-60.05484pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 29.79292pt}}_{{\kern-29.40404pt\kern 29.79292pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-60.05484pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 29.79292pt}}_{{\kern-29.40404pt\kern 29.79292pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-45.6601pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 19.60272pt}}_{{\kern-19.21384pt\kern 19.60272pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega(g_{2},g_{3})^{{\kern-41.73523pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.67786pt}}_{{\kern-15.28897pt\kern 15.67786pt}}}\cdot\Omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\cdot P_{edge}\,.

(S103)

As we have already seen that
\Omega
’s can be decomposed into boundary terms,
\Omega_{a}
and
\Omega_{b}
at the two ends of the segment, then we have the condition that
\Omega_{a}
and
\Omega_{b}
should satisfy:

\displaystyle\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{b}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})=\frac{1}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}

\displaystyle{}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{b}(g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})

(S104)

\displaystyle(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})}\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{a}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\frac{1}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}

\displaystyle={}^{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\Omega_{a}(g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{a}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})(-)^{\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})}.

We can now check Eq. (
46
) directly using the explicit form of
\Omega_{a/b}
. Here only the case of
\Omega_{b}
is checked in details, while
\Omega_{a}
’s case can be checked in a quite similar way.
The left hand side of the equation above is

\displaystyle\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2})\Omega_{b}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{edge}\cdot w_{L}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2})\hbox to56.13pt{\vbox to9.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 28.06218pt\lower-4.89929pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})n_{{}_{f,L}}}\,\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\cdot P_{edge}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{edge}\cdot w_{L}(g_{1})\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-36.69159pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-27.33765pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{2})^{{\kern-24.49083pt{K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{3})^{{\kern-40.73654pt{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{3})^{{\kern-40.73654pt{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\kern 23.1606pt}}_{{\kern-22.77171pt\kern 23.1606pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{3})^{{\kern-31.3826pt{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\kern 15.09583pt}}_{{\kern-14.70694pt\kern 15.09583pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}}}}w_{L}(g_{3})^{{\kern-28.53578pt{K^{s(g_{1}g_{2})}}\kern 12.24901pt}}_{{\kern-11.86012pt\kern 12.24901pt}}}w^{-1}_{L}(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3})\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}(\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})+\alpha(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}))\,n_{{}_{f,L}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\,\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})}}}

(S105)

where the additional exponential factor in blue color is due to rearranging terms according to the blue arrow in the equation above, while such factor can be read out from the relation Eq. (

).
The right hand side is a little more complicated:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-35.97028pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-35.97028pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-29.59627pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}}}}\Omega^{{\kern-28.58514pt{U_{M}(g_{1})K^{s(g_{1})}}\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}}_{b}(g_{2},g_{3})\Omega_{b}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{edge}\cdot\left(w_{L}(g_{1})\hbox to102.91pt{\vbox to11.38pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 51.45262pt\lower-5.68945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}}}\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}(g_{1})}\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})n_{f_{f,L}}}\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\cdot P_{edge}

\displaystyle(-)^{n^{(2)}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{2},g_{3})\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\cdot P_{edge}\,.\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

(S106)

Now we can conclude the relation between
\omega
and
\lambda
’s by comparing Eq. (
S104
), (
S105
), and (
S106
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})}\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2})\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})n_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}(g_{1})}}{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}(-)^{n^{(2)}_{\lambda,\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3})}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})}\right)\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-9.61713pt{g_{1}}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-4.35419pt\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0144pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-2.46945pt\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{g_{1}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.19774pt{g_{1}}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-1.65279pt\kern 2.04167pt}}}^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{2},g_{3})\lambda^{(2)}_{\frac{1}{2}}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\,.

(S107)

Now we can compare Eq. (
S107
) and (
S54
), then the relation between
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
can be read out:

\displaystyle\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})=\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\exp\big[-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})+\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\big]\,.

(S108)

We have already known the 3-cocycle condition that
\omega^{\prime}
satisfies, then the calculation of 3-cocycle condition for
\omega
are simplified by calculating those exponential factors:

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})[\omega(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{\omega(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})[\omega^{\prime}(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{\omega^{\prime}(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\omega^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})}}}

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}}{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})}}}\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\operatorname{i}(-)^{s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{-\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{2})+s(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})\sigma(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}}}

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}}}\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{-\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}}{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{+\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})}}}\,.

(S109)

Merging terms that are in the same color together, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\omega(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})[\omega(g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})]^{(-)^{s(g_{1})}}}{\omega(g_{1}g_{2},g_{3},g_{4})\omega(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}g_{4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}~\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})}\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\operatorname{i}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\left[(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})-\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\right]}}}

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\left[\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})-\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})\right]}}}\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\operatorname{i}\sigma(g_{1}g_{2}g_{3},g_{4})\left[\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})-(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})\right]}}}

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1})}\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})\left[\alpha(g_{2},g_{3})-(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})\right]}}}\times\mathrm{e}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\tilde{\rho}(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{3},g_{4})\left[\sigma(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3}g_{4})-\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\right]}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-)^{\sigma(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}~\mathrm{e}^{\operatorname{i}(-)^{\rho(g_{1}g_{2})}\alpha(g_{1},g_{2})\sigma(g_{3},g_{4})}\,,

(S110)

thus we recover 3-cocycle condition that we obtained from the anomalous symmetry actions on edge in Section
III
from tensor equations.

VII
Details about Kasteleyn orientation

VII.1
Kasteleyn orientation and symmetric FPEPS

In this section, we define rules to extract an orientation from orders of symmetry actions on internal legs and prove that the physical wavefunction is
g
-symmetric only when the right hand side of Eq. (
164
) is a Kasteleyn orientation.
According to Eq. (
69
), the commutator between
W(g)
and parity
F\equiv\exp[\operatorname{i}\pi n_{f}]
reads

\displaystyle W(g)\cdot F\cdot W^{-1}(g)=D(g)\cdot F

(S111)

where
D(g)=\exp[\operatorname{i}\pi n_{D}(g)]
, whose eigenvalues equal
\pm 1
.
Thus,
D(g)=P_{e}(g)-P_{o}(g)
, where
P_{e/o}
are projectors to subspace with eigenvalue
\pm 1
.

With these projectors, we can decompose site and bond tensors to orthogonal sectors, e.g.
P_{e,x}(g)\otimes_{f}P_{o,y}(g)\otimes_{f}P_{o,z}(g)\cdot\hat{T}
.
By inserting
P_{e}(g)+P_{o}(g)
on all internal legs of the tensor network wavefunction, the wavefunction equals summation of tensor contractions for different sectors.
However, as
D(g)
is an IGG element, local tensors vanish if acted by odd number of
P_{o}(g)
’s.
In other words, action of
P_{o}(g)
must form loops on the network.
Given a loop configuration
d
, by acting
P_{o}(g)
’s on internal legs along loops and
P_{e}(g)
’s on internal legs within domains, we obtain site tensors
\hat{T}^{d}
’s and bond tensors
\hat{B}^{d}
’s.
By contracting internal legs of
\hat{T}^{d}
’s and
\hat{B}^{d}
’s, we get physical state
\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
, and physical wavefunction can be expressed as
\lvert\Psi\rangle=\sum_{d}\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
.

In the following, we will prove that
\forall d,~g\circ\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle=\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
if orders of
W(g)
action gives Kasteleyn orientation, and thus
\lvert\Psi\rangle
is invariant under
g
.

We start from configuration
d
without loop.
In this case, we only get
W_{e}(g)
’s when acting
g
, which are parity even and free to permute.

\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
is apparently invariant under
g
.

For configuration
d
with a single loop, the situation is similar to Section
VI.1
.
Let the number of internal ket legs to be
2L
, internal legs along this loop are labeled counter-clockwise by
l
, where site tensors sit between
l=2k-1
and
l=2k
.
Site tensors are then named as
\hat{T}_{2k-1,2k}^{d}
, while bond tensors as
\hat{B}_{2k,2k+1}^{d}
.
As all tensors are parity even, we rearrange tensors along the loop together in the following way:

\displaystyle\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle={}

\displaystyle{\rm{fTr}}[\cdots\otimes_{f}\hat{B}_{2,3}^{d}\otimes_{f}\hat{B}_{3,4}^{d}\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}\hat{B}_{2L,1}^{d}\otimes_{f}

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{2L-1,2L}^{d}\otimes_{f}\hat{T}^{d}_{2L-3,2L-2}\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}\hat{T}_{1,2}^{d}\otimes_{f}\cdots]

(S112)

We now act
g
on
\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
, and according to Eq. (
165
) and (
167
), it equals action of
W(g)
’s on internal legs of
\hat{T}^{d}
’s and
\hat{B}^{d}
’s.
We define
W_{e/o}(g)\equiv P_{e/o}\cdot W(g)
, which is parity even/odd sector of
W(\mathcal{T})
.
From definition of
\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
,
W(g)
’s act as
W_{o}(g)
’s on internal legs along the loop, while acting as
W_{e}(g)
’s on internal legs away from the loop.
Fermion signs come from permuting
W_{o}(g)
’s on the loop.
We arrange the order of
W_{o}(g)
’s contraction according to Eq. (
S112
) as

\displaystyle{\rm{fTr}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\Big((-1)^{s_{2,3}}\cdot W_{o,2}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{o,3}(g)\Big)\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}\Big((-1)^{s_{2L,1}}\cdot W_{o,2L}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{o,1}(g)\Big)\bigotimes\mathop{{}_{\mkern-4.0muf}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\Big((-1)^{s_{1,2}}\cdot W_{o,1}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{o,2}(g)\Big)\otimes_{f}\cdots\otimes_{f}\Big((-1)^{s_{2L-1,2L}}\cdot W_{o,2L-1}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{o,2L}(g)\Big)\bigg]^{-1}\bigg\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(-1)^{1+\sum_{l}s_{l,l+1}}=1

(S113)

where
s_{l,l+1}=0/1
if the arrow at
(l,l+1)
is along/against the direction of the loop (counter-clockwise/clockwise direction).
The last line in Eq. (
S113
) is from the definition of Kasteleyn orientation.
So, for
d
with a single loop,
W(g)
’s and
[W(g)]^{-1}
’s cancels, and
\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
is
g
-symmetric.
In contrast, if the orientation is not Kasteleyn, one can always find a loop configuration
d
, such that the last line of Eq. (
S113
) gives
-1
.
So,
\lvert\Psi\rangle
breaks
g
symmetry if Kasteleyn orientation is not satisfied.

For configuration
d
with more than one loops, we can arrange all tensors belonging to one loop together, and repeat the above calculation for every loop.
Thus, such
\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
is also
g
-symmetric.
In conclusion,
\lvert\Psi\rangle
is
g
-symmetric if and only if the orientation extracted from
W(g)
’s is a Kasteleyn orientation.

As we mentioned in Section
VI.1
, by flipping arrows on all edges connecting to certain vertices, one gets another Kasteleyn orientation.
In the tensor language, flipping arrows for edges connecting to vertex
(s\alpha)
corresponds to modifying
W_{(s\alpha)}(g)
to
D_{(s\alpha)}(g)\cdot W_{(s\alpha)}(g)
:

\displaystyle\left[D_{(sx)}(g)\cdot W_{(sx)}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{(sy)}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{(sz)}(g)\right]^{-1}\cdot\hat{T}_{s}=\left[W_{(sy)}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{(sz)}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{(sx)}\right]^{-1}\cdot\hat{T}_{s}~~~\Rightarrow\scalebox{2}[2]{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{}}}}}}$}}

Similar logic works for bond tensors.
Following these rules for arrows, there is one-to-one correspondence between gauge transformation
W(g)
and Kasteleyn orientation.

The above argument can be easily generalized to any trivalent lattice, where each vertex connects three bonds, and thus there is at most one loop travelling through a vertex.
We now consider generic graphs, where more than one loops may meet at sites.

Let us present rules for extract orientations in generic lattices.
Similar as Eq. (
164
), we first construct a new planar graph, where a site connecting
n
-bonds in the original lattice splits to
n
vertices in the new graph, and each vertex is labeled by the corresponding internal leg index.
Each pair of these
n
vertices are connected by new edges.
Given
g
-action on internal legs, arrows on edges of the new planar graph follows similar rules presented in Eq. (
166
) and Eq. (
167
).
For example, consider site tensor
s
with four internal legs, the above rule reads

\displaystyle U(g)K^{s(g)}\cdot\hat{T}_{s}=\left[W_{(sa)}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{(sb)}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{(sc)}\otimes_{f}W_{(sd)}\right]^{-1}\cdot\hat{T}_{s}~~~\Rightarrow\scalebox{2}[2]{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{}}}}}}$}}

To proceed, let us focus on a particular choice of Kasteleyn orientation.
We number the
n
vertices from
1
to
n
clockwise, and let the arrow points from
i
to
j
if
i<j
.
It is easy to verify that any loop within these
n
vertices matches the condition for Kasteleyn orientation.
Arrows on bond tensors are chosen to satisfy conditions for Kasteleyn orientation with larger loops.

For loop configurations without loop crossing, using similar argument presented in the honeycomb lattice case, we conclude that
W(g)
’s and
[W(g)]^{-1}
’s cancels without additional fermion sign.

Figure S4:
Two domain wall loops (red and blue) intersect at site
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
.
Here,
s_{1,2}
are not “true crossing points” of these two loops: they are separate at these two points.

.

As shown in Fig.
S4
, we consider configuration
d
where two loops (colored blue and red) intersect at site
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
.
Loops are chosen such that internal legs belonging to one loop are neighbours.
Thus, there are no “true crossing points” between these two loops.

By inserting
P_{o}
’s on internal legs at these two loops, and
P_{e}
’s on other internal legs, we obtain tensors
\hat{T}^{d}
’s and
\hat{B}^{d}
’s, and wavefunction
\lvert\psi_{d}\rangle
from contracting
\hat{T}^{d}
’s and
\hat{B}^{d}
’s.
Let the number of internal ket legs of the red/blue loop to be
2L_{1}/2L_{2}
.
We label internal legs along these two loops by index
l
, where
1\leq l\leq 2L_{1}
labels internal legs along the red loop and
2L_{1}+1\leq l\leq 2L_{1}+2L_{2}
for the blue loop.
Due to the intersecting sites
s_{1/2}
, Eq. (
S112
) do not directly apply to the case here.
However, at any intersection point, two
W_{o}^{-1}(g)
’s belonging to one loop can be always moved together without sign, and in this way, Eq. (
S113
) still applies for every single loop.
The above argument can be easily generalized to any loop configurations.
We then conclude that for a
g
-symmetric FPEPS, the planar graph extracted from
W(g)
’s satisfies Kasteleyn orientation.

VII.2
Symmetry on the open boundary from Kasteleyn orientation

We now consider a finite 2D FPEPS on region
A
, which is cut from an infinite FPEPS, as shown in Fig.
7
. The aim of this section is to show the validity of Eqn. (
169
).
By contracting all internal legs within
A
, we obtain a large tensor
\hat{T}_{A}
:

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{A}={\rm{fTr}}_{A}[\mathbb{B}\otimes_{f}\mathbb{T}]\,,

(S114)

We now act
g
on physical legs of
\hat{T}_{A}
, which give
W(g)
’s and
W^{-1}(g)
’s on all bulk and boundary virtual legs of
\hat{T}_{A}
according to Eq. (
165
) and (
167
). The parity of
W(g)
’s are determined by
D(g)
’s which depend on the string configuration. Now consider a configuration
d
that there is only one string with ends
i
and
j
, according to Eqn. (
169
), the boundary symmetry action
W^{d}_{\partial A}
is

\displaystyle W^{d}_{\partial A}(g)=(-1)^{\xi(j)-\xi(i)}\mathbb{W}_{e}\otimes_{f}W_{i}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{j}(g)

(S115)

Where
\mathbb{W}_{e}
is the tensor product of parity even
W(g)
’s acting other boundary internal legs. Now we turn to the
1
D string
L=\{l_{1},l_{2},\dots,l_{n}\}
with ends
l_{1}=i
and
l_{n}=j
. As parity even
W(g)
’s and
W(g)^{-1}
’s acting on other bulk internal legs already cancel, we can read the symmetry equation according to Eqn. (
162
):

\displaystyle U_{A}(g)\cdot\hat{T}^{d}_{A}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\sum s(l_{i},l_{i+1})}{\rm{fTr}}[\mathbb{W}_{e}\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{l_{3}}(g)\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{l_{2}}(g)\otimes_{f}\dots\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{l_{n-1}}\otimes_{f}W^{-1}_{l_{n-2}}\otimes_{f}W_{l_{1}}(g)\otimes_{f}\dots\otimes_{f}W_{l_{n}}(g)\otimes_{f}\hat{T}^{d}_{A}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\sum s(l_{i},l_{i+1})}{\rm{fTr}}[\mathbb{W}_{e}\otimes_{f}W_{l_{1}}(g)\otimes_{f}W_{l_{n}}(g)\otimes_{f}\hat{T}^{d}_{A}]

\displaystyle={\rm{fTr}}[W_{\partial A}^{d}(g)\otimes_{f}\hat{T}^{d}_{A}]

(S116)

Where
\hat{T}^{d}_{A}
is the tensor
\hat{T}_{A}
with the specific domain configuration
d
.
We know from Kasteleyn orientation that
[\xi(j)-\xi(i)]+\sum s(l_{i},l_{i+1})=0\mod 2
, thus Eqn. (
S115
) is valid, which means Eqn. (
169
) is valid for one open string case. The verification of multi-string case is straightforward as bulk
W(g)
’s and
W^{-1}(g)
’s from different strings are always separable as shown in Fig.
S4
. Thus for multiple strings case, the
W^{d}_{\partial A}
is

\displaystyle W^{d}_{\partial A}(g)=(-1)^{\sum_{\alpha}\xi(l^{(\alpha)}_{n})-\xi(l^{(\alpha)}_{1})}\mathbb{W}_{e}\bigotimes_{\alpha}{}_{f}W^{(\alpha)}_{l_{1}}(g)\otimes_{f}W^{(\alpha)}_{l_{n}}(g)

(S117)

Here
\alpha
denotes different strings. Thus, Eqn. (
169
) is valid for all configurations.
